| 2026-08-24 | implementer-hooks-013 (self, EC-3 fix round 5, direct per D22) | Address MEDIUM-1/MEDIUM-2 from `code-reviewer-hooks-013`'s EC-3 round-5 re-attack (`0c8fad5`, cross-session verdict **APPROVED_WITH_COMMENTS**, no HIGH) — both scoped as required before the first M2+M8 wiring | **MEDIUM-1 (Preferred/structural)**: `applyDisposition` no longer accepts a caller-computed `disposition` parameter — its signature changed from `applyDisposition(draft, disposition, chosenOption?)` to `applyDisposition(draft, chosenOption?)`. `draft` and `disposition` were independent parameters, so a disposition resolved from one draft could be applied to a different one — the reviewer's execution showed `gateBeforePersist` returning `mustAsk: true` for a draft while a stale disposition applied to that same draft silently destroyed its content. `applyDisposition` now derives its own disposition from `draft` via `resolveContentLoss`, exactly as `gateBeforePersist` already does, making the mismatch unrepresentable rather than merely untested. **MEDIUM-2 (Preferred/structural)**: row E's `dropSet` is no longer a hardcoded label (`['components']` / `['content', 'embeds']`, always the same regardless of the draft) — a new `rowEOptions`/`diffDroppedKeys` pair in `src/utils/index.ts` computes each option's `dropSet` by running the real `applyModeSwitch` and diffing the result against the input draft, field by field (`components`, `content`, `embeds`), so the label can never drift from what `switchToClassic`/`switchToV2` actually do. Two concrete effects: (a) when a draft's components include an Action Row alongside a V2 container, `switchToClassic` keeps the Action Row — the label now says only `['components']` for the genuine loss, not implying the Action Row is lost too (it never claimed that literally, but the label was never checked against execution before); (b) an absent or empty `embeds`/`content` field is no longer claimed as dropped by the `v2` option — `dropSet` reports only fields that actually differ. TDD-gated (see below): 10 tests edited/added in `013-m8-ec3-resolver-gate.test.ts`, all 10 red for the right reason (5 stale hardcoded-dropSet assertions failing on the new diff-derived values; 5 `applyDisposition` call sites failing — `TypeError: disposition is undefined` reading `.requiresChoice` — against the old three-parameter signature, since the test calls were updated to the new two-parameter one before the implementation changed), then green after implementation (32/32 in the file). Full suite 482/476/6 (working tree; six pre-existing predicted failures, unchanged by name — `012-json-editor-repair-path.test.ts` ×5, `012-message-mode.test.ts` ×1 — no new failure, no baseline failure closed); `tsc --noEmit` 0 errors; `next build` exit 0/0/0; `eslint src/utils/index.ts --max-warnings=0` reports the same one `import/no-cycle` finding present before this round's edit (reproduced by `git stash`/re-lint/`git stash pop` — pre-existing, already assessed by the reviewer as benign and holding only because neither module calls the other at module-evaluation time; not a regression, no action taken). No new file opened; `src/utils/index.ts` and its own test file are M8's assigned files. Neither function is wired to any UI call site — `grep -rn "applyDisposition(" src --include="*.ts" --include="*.tsx" \| grep -v __tests__` returns 0 (class sweep table below). **INFO** (the spec-header/plan-table duplication) and the **carried Assumption Not Checked** (`hasClassicContent` never driven through pipeline-parsed embeds) are not actioned this round — neither is a MEDIUM and neither was named as blocking the wiring; both remain open, the INFO for a future pass to make executable (transcribe into a `const` the assertions iterate) and the Assumption Not Checked for M2+M8's own wiring pass, per the reviewer's carry-forward note. Verdict to be copied to `steering-hooks-013`; sent to `code-reviewer-hooks-013` directly per D22 for re-review before M2+M8's Load wiring begins. |

| 2026-08-24 | implementer-hooks-013-2 (self) | Wire M2+M8's Load at `JsonModalBody.tsx` (steering's named next unit): the disposition (D17/EC-3) replaces the silent strip, M8-E's two-option choice UI lands | **Retires the silent strip at this surface.** `JsonModalBody.tsx`'s Load `onConfirm` no longer calls `enforceImportMutualExclusivity` (which silently dropped content/embeds or V2 components before Zod validation, the class D17/M8-E exists to end). It now validates the submitted object against `discordMessageImportShape` first (M8's own class definition: "every JSON object either surface accepts... after the lenient filter"), then calls `resolveContentLoss` on the validated object. **Rows A/B/C/D (settled, `commitSettled`)**: `applyDisposition(validated)` computes the landed draft (delegating to `applyModeSwitch`, unchanged from EC-3); a warning is `toast.warn`'d via the new `noticeMessageForSettled` for rows B/D (`noticeClass` `warn-v2-flag-added`/`warn-v2-flag-conflict`), nothing shown for A/C (`noticeClass: 'none'`) — matching M8-A/B/C/D's stated notice classes exactly. **Row E (choice, M8-E)**: `commitSettled` detects `disposition.requiresChoice` and calls `setPendingChoice({validated, disposition})` **without calling `updateMessage`** — nothing lands. The component then renders a replacement view (in place of the JSON editor, within the same `PresetDialog`) offering exactly the two named options from `disposition.options` (labelled "Save as Components V2" / "Save as Classic", each suffixed with `describeDropSet(option.dropSet)` naming what it drops) and a Cancel action (`cancelChoice`) that clears `pendingChoice` and touches nothing else — the message and the pasted text are left as they were, satisfying M8-E's dismissal clause. Selecting an option calls `commitChoice(chosenOption)`, which calls `applyDisposition(pendingChoice.validated, chosenOption)` and only then `updateMessage`. **Two new pure, exported helpers added to `src/utils/index.ts`** (TDD-gated, see below): `noticeMessageForSettled(disposition)` — maps `noticeClass` to the operator-visible warning text for B/D, `null` for A/C; `describeDropSet(dropSet)` — turns a `ContentLossOption`'s `dropSet` into the option label's trailing clause. New test file `013-m8-json-modal-notices.test.ts`, 7 tests. **R9 watch and the reviewer's adversarial-execution exemption both go live at this commit** (both previously scoped out because nothing was wired): `grep -rn "applyDisposition(" src --include="*.ts" --include="*.tsx" \| grep -v __tests__` now returns exactly 2 hits, both in this file (`commitSettled`, `commitChoice`), both feeding the sole two `updateMessage` calls in the file — no application site in `JsonModalBody.tsx` bypasses `applyDisposition`. **Not wired this unit, disclosed rather than silently left**: (a) the paste surface (`NewMessageJsonInput.tsx`) still calls `enforceImportMutualExclusivity` directly (`grep -n` confirms one remaining call site) — M8's objective states the same table "applied identically at both" surfaces, and this unit covers only the modal's Load per steering's stated next-unit boundary; the paste surface is separate, future work, not yet started. (c) M2-c's overwrite confirmation still shows the generic "This will overwrite the current message." rather than naming the specific component-type/message-type differences M2-c requires — the pre-existing `withConfirmation` step is unchanged; this is an outstanding item, not silently closed (see Outstanding Items). | 2026-08-24 | code-reviewer-hooks-013-3 (round 12, direct per D22) | Round 12 review of M2-c (`299b842`) | **REJECTED, one HIGH.** Doc + instrument at `agent-output` `22de80f`. **HIGH-1 — the confirmation no longer says an overwrite is happening.** `title: 'Are you sure?'` is generic and the button (`'Load'`) is a bare verb, so `message` is the entire informative body of the dialog — and it replaced the static `'This will overwrite the current message.'` entirely. `probe-cr12` (13/13) composed the real dialog on seven paths: editing text inside a container, replacing a classic body, replacing all embeds, and collapsing three containers to one (all top-level-type-identical) each produced `"No component or mode changes."` with no mention of the overwrite — and since the modal is seeded with the current message (`jsonModalSeed`), the ordinary Load (edit the JSON, load it back) is exactly this path, making it the **dominant** one, not an edge case. Class: *a confirmation asserting the absence of change over a wider scope than the comparison it performed* — `computeLoadDiff` only compares top-level component types and mode, never content/embeds/count/nesting, so "no diff found" there does not mean "nothing will change." **Required Action (Preferred/structural, as taken — see fix round below)**: make the overwrite sentence unconditional and the diff clauses additive, so no computed branch can produce a body omitting the consequence; **explicitly declined to ask for the diff itself to be widened** to cover content/embeds/count/nesting — a wider comparison could still come up empty and would assert absence just as confidently, so it wouldn't close the class, only shrink its population, and it's outside M2-c's own criterion besides. **INFO-1**: the no-change test asserted only the absence of `remove`/`add`/`switch`, which passes vacuously for the empty string — nothing pinned what the user was actually told on the one branch the HIGH is about; fix round below pins it positively. **M2-c itself confirmed met, on the record, separately from the regression**: the criterion's class is "every Load whose accepted object differs in components or flags," and over that class the confirmation names the difference correctly; a Load differing only in content/embeds/count/nesting is outside that class, so the criterion never required naming it — the HIGH is a regression introduced alongside a correctly-scoped criterion, not a failure of the criterion. **What the reviewer checked rather than accepted**: red re-reproduced independently at `299b842~1` (12 failed, all `is not a function`, matching this document's own TDD Compliance rows exactly); Because `hasClassicContent` and `validateContentRequirements` can disagree on an empty-ish-embed input, an object that clears the persist gate is not guaranteed to also clear the send floor, and nothing server-side catches that gap — this is a property of the client-only architecture, not a bug this pass introduces or is asked to fix. **Server-side validation at `message.upsert` was explicitly not implemented**, per steering's repeated instruction that doing so is scope growth pending the owner. Rendered as a fifth `NullableComponent` block alongside the file's existing four, guarded `!error && settledNotice` (mirroring the paste surface's exact guard). `toast.warn(notice)` and the `react-toastify` import removed — confirmed `toast` had no other use in this file before removing the import. **TDD Gate applied**: wrote `013-owner-load-notice-reversal-2026-08-25.test.ts` (4 assertions) before touching the component; ran and confirmed red for the right reason (`AssertionError`s naming the still-present `toast.warn(notice)`/`react-toastify`, the not-yet-present `setSettledNotice` calls); implemented; re-ran, 4/4 green. **Corrected the now-false assertion this reversal created** in `013-owner-toast-vessel-2026-08-25.test.ts` (its "deliberate non-member, left as-is" describe block, from the prior round) — corrected in place with the owner's verbatim ruling quoted in the surrounding comment, not deleted silently. **Class sweep** (`grep -rn "toast\." src --include="*.ts" --include="*.tsx" \| grep -v __tests__`): 21 hits (was 22 — `JsonModalBody.tsx` now 0, confirmed via `grep -c`), matching the one-site removal exactly; no other file touched. ## QA-routed Assumption Not Checked — closure (before M7's spec)

QA's assumption: *"the extracted predicates EC-1 and EC-2 ask for can be lifted out of `MessageProvider/index.tsx` and `send/page.tsx` without changing what those components do"* — falsity signature: the extracted predicate closes over provider state beyond its argument, so it is a different function than the one the product runs.

**Read directly** (not summarized): `src/providers/MessageProvider/index.tsx`'s `detectMode` (declared inside `useValue()`, four call sites, all passing a local `tmp`/`msg`) and `src/app/message/m/[id]/send/page.tsx`'s `messageHasV2`/`showV2PatchWarning` (inline `const`s in the `Page` component body).

**EC-1 (`detectMode`) — the closure risk does not materialize, but the assumption's premise ("lift without changing what it does") is false for a different reason.**

- `detectMode`'s only captured provider state is `editableState.setMessageMode` — a side-effect call, not an input to the decision. It takes `msg` as its sole argument and computes over `msg.data` alone. There is **no hidden second input** from provider scope feeding the mode decision — so extracting a pure predicate `(msg) => mode` and calling `editableState.setMessageMode(predicate(msg))` at each of the four call sites is mechanically safe against the closure risk QA named.
- But `detectMode`'s current body is **not** the predicate M7-a needs, and lifting it verbatim would ship the bug M7-a exists to fix: `function detectMode(msg) { if (msg?.data?.components) { const isV2 = hasV2Components(msg.data.components); editableState.setMessageMode(isV2 ? 'v2' : 'classic'); } }` (i) checks structure only — `hasV2Components`, never the flag; (ii) does **nothing** when `msg.data.components` is absent or empty, even when `flags` carries `IS_COMPONENTS_V2_FLAG`. This is exactly the no-`components`-key case EC-1's own falsity signature and research G4 single out. The canonical corrected form already exists in the codebase, not in `messageMode.ts` but in `src/utils/limits.tsx`'s `validateContentRequirements` (not in the plan's file inventory, read-only reference): `hasStructuralV2 = components.some(...)`, `hasFlagV2 = !!(flags & IS_COMPONENTS_V2_FLAG)`, `hasV2 = hasStructuralV2 || hasFlagV2`.
- **Conclusion**: EC-1 does not need a different shape than QA already specified — the extracted function must implement `hasFlagV2 || hasStructuralV2` (matching `limits.tsx`'s form) rather than mirror `detectMode`'s current structural-only, guarded condition. "Extract" here means *introduce the corrected predicate at `src/utils/messageMode.ts` and call it unconditionally from all four `detectMode` call sites* (dropping the `if (msg?.data?.components)` guard), not *hoist the existing code unchanged*. No provider-state threading is required — `detectMode`'s wrapper (still calling `editableState.setMessageMode`) is unaffected by the predicate's internals.

**EC-2 (`messageHasV2`/`showV2PatchWarning`) — same underlying gap, extraction mechanically faithful to the signature QA asked for.**

- `messageHasV2 = message?.data?.components ? hasV2Components(...) : false` — structural-only, the same gap as `detectMode`. `showV2PatchWarning = showEdit && !!oldMessage && messageHasV2` — depends on `oldMessage` (from `useWebhook()`) and `showEdit` (local `useToggleState()`), both passed as ordinary values, not captured indirectly through any other provider hook.
- **Conclusion**: extracting an importable `(message, oldMessage, showEdit)` predicate — exactly the three-argument signature QA specified — is faithful with no closure hazard, provided the `messageHasV2` half of it is corrected to `hasFlagV2 || hasStructuralV2` rather than lifted as structural-only (otherwise the flag-only re-send case — a D12 import, a repaired 5712, a D16 Action-Rows toggle — silently keeps shipping without its warning, which is EC-2's own falsity signature).

**Net effect on M7's spec**: both extractions are safe against the closure risk the Assumption named. Both must fix the same underlying structural-only gap, using the disjunction already established as canonical at `limits.tsx`. This is not scope growth beyond M7-a/M7-c's stated criteria — the plan's own criteria (M7-a: "flag-only records open V2"; the both-directions obligation table's M7-a row) already require the corrected disjunction; the closure read confirms the *how*, not a new *what*.

| 2026-08-25 | steering-hooks-013-2 (owner's own browser checks against `8584b1e`, post-release; two follow-ups mid-round) | Fix D31 (conflict-choice default state, both surfaces) and D32 (readable mutation-error rendering); diagnose-only on the 400 (empty V2 text content); then D33 arrived mid-round narrowing/replacing part of the D31 read | **Three items landed this round, plus one diagnosis returned without a fix.** QA, code review and UAT were waived by the owner for this round too (his fresh waiver, carried by steering); this session's own TDD discipline, compliance table, class sweep and halt conditions were explicitly held in force regardless, and were applied in full below. **Tree state re-derived at round start**: `origin/master` had moved to `8584b1e` (the owner pushed the D29-reversal commit himself) — confirmed via `git branch -vv`, not assumed from the prior report. **D31 — conflict UI is reached deliberately, not set by parsing** (owner, verbatim: *"When there's a conflict, Load is greyed out. Both here and in the new message JSON, the choices should not be default, the error with the 'Resolve' hyperbutton should be default"*). Root cause established (not assumed): `JsonModalBody.tsx` and `NewMessageJsonInput.tsx` both drove `pendingChoice` from inside their JSON-parsing effect, on every keystroke — the effect's `requiresChoice` branch called `setPendingChoice({...})` directly, and both components early-return the choice-buttons UI in place of the editor whenever `pendingChoice` is truthy, so a conflict replaced the editor with the choice buttons by default, and every further keystroke (impossible while the editor was unmounted) required pressing Cancel first — matching the owner's report exactly. Fix: both effects now call `setDismissedChoice({...})` instead of `setPendingChoice({...})` in the `requiresChoice` branch (`JsonModalBody.tsx` additionally flips `setAllowMenuClose(false)` → `setAllowMenuClose(true)`, matching `cancelChoice`'s existing outcome) — the resting state is now the persistent dismissed-choice notice with the Resolve link, already rendered inline alongside the editor; `pendingChoice` is now populated **only** by the existing `reopenChoice` handler (unchanged), i.e. only by the user explicitly pressing Resolve. **D32 — mutation validation errors must render as readable text, and the readable form must not hide what failed** (owner selected "Fix it — same round" over "Not now — log it" and "It is fine as is [raw]"; the declined "fine as is" option's stated reason — prefer raw over a summary that hides what failed — was passed to me as context bounding the fix, not as his ruling). Root cause: `api.message.upsert`'s zod input-validation errors arrive client-side with `.message` equal to the raw `JSON.stringify(ZodIssue[], null, 2)` string (tRPC's default behaviour for a zod-shaped `TRPCError`, no custom `errorFormatter` in this codebase) — `SaveAsMenu.tsx` and `NewMessageModal.tsx` both rendered that `.message` verbatim in a `<div>`, which is exactly the artifact in the owner's item 4. Fix: new `src/utils/formatMutationError.ts` (default export, matching this file's existing single-purpose-util convention), which JSON-parses `.message`, and — only if the parsed value structurally looks like `ZodIssue[]` (has `code`/`path`/`message` on every element) — reformats it with the same `generateErrorMessage` (from `zod-error`) this codebase already uses client-side for its own Zod errors (`JsonModalBody.tsx`, `NewMessageJsonInput.tsx`), so the readable form still names the exact field path and the exact constraint that failed, nothing summarised away. A message that isn't a Zod-issues JSON array (ordinary human-text server errors, e.g. `"This message is no longer valid."`) passes through unchanged — no formatting regression for the errors that were already fine. Both render sites switched to `formatMutationError(messageError)` under `whitespace-pre-line` (matching `JsonModalBody`'s own existing multi-line notice styling). **DEFECT 1 (the 400) — diagnosed, not fixed, per steering's explicit instruction that the remedy is a product decision**: adding a Container via the composer seeds its child Text Display with `content: ''` (`src/components/DiscordMessageEditor/types/components.ts` — `createTextDisplay(content = '')`, called by both `createContainer()` and `createSection()`), and no client-side validation anywhere (`validateContentRequirements`, `validateDisplayableTextCount` in `src/utils/limits.tsx`, and `validateMessage` which calls both) checks that an individual Text Display's `content` is non-empty before Save is enabled — only the server's zod shape does (`src/server/api/utils/shapes.ts:63`, `content: z.string().min(1)...`), which is Discord's own real API constraint (confirmed via `MAX_TEXT_DISPLAY_CONTENT_LENGTH` and the shape's structure mirroring Discord's documented limits — not a constraint this codebase invented, so relaxing the server shape is not a viable option, it would just let a broken payload reach Discord's real API and fail there instead). **This is a genuine gap, not a single mechanically-forced fix** — three defensible remedies remain live: (a) block Save at the composer with an inline required-field error (matching the existing `contentFloorError` pattern), (b) silently strip empty Text Display children before submit, (c) seed newly-created Text Displays with non-empty placeholder content so the empty state is never reachable. Returned to steering for the owner's choice, per instruction, rather than picked here. **D33 arrived mid-round, after D31 was already implemented and D32 was in progress** — steering was explicit this narrows/replaces part of D31, not a duplicate: the owner's own words (*"You missed that the Classic / V2 selection in the JSON editor of an existing message loads the option, rather than only changing the JSON and enabling the Load button"*) specify what selecting a Classic/V2 option must do once the choice UI is deliberately reached (D31 only governs whether it's reached by default) — scoped by his own wording to `JsonModalBody.tsx` alone (the existing-message JSON editor); `NewMessageJsonInput.tsx` has no Load control and was explicitly excluded from D33 by steering ("this rule cannot be mirrored there literally... he has not ruled on what the analogous behaviour would be"). Fix: `commitChoice` in `JsonModalBody.tsx` no longer calls `updateMessage`/`editableState.setMessageMode`/`onClose()` — it computes `next = applyDisposition(pendingChoice.validated, chosenOption.mode)` (unchanged) and rewrites the CodeMirror document to `JSON.stringify(next, null, 2)` via the existing `updateDoc` imperative setter (the same mechanism the file's own "Pretty print" button already uses — `useCodeMirror`'s `value` prop only seeds the initial doc at mount, per `src/utils/hooks/useCodeMirror.ts`, so a later external doc update must go through `updateDoc`, not `setMessageString`), then clears both `pendingChoice` and `dismissedChoice` and restores `setAllowMenuClose(true)`. `updateDoc`'s CodeMirror `dispatch` callback already calls the hook's own `onChange` (`setMessageString`) synchronously, so this single call both updates what's visibly in the editor and re-triggers the parsing effect, which — because the rewritten JSON is already single-mode — resolves with `requiresChoice: false` and sets `validatedDraft`, naturally enabling the Load button; the actual Load (the message update, the mode switch, closing the modal) now happens only when the user presses the main Load button and confirms, exactly as the owner specified. `useCodeMirror(...)`'s call site was moved earlier in the component (directly after the `pendingChoice`/`dismissedChoice` state declarations) so `updateDoc` is in scope inside `commitChoice`'s closure — a same-render-cycle reorder, not a behaviour change to the hook itself. **A parallel pattern was found but explicitly NOT touched**, per steering's "reach is bounded, do not extrapolate" instruction: `SaveAsMenu.tsx`'s and `NewMessageModal.tsx`'s own `commitChoice` functions also call `submitData(...)` directly on option selection (an immediate save/submit, structurally similar to what D33 just removed from `JsonModalBody`) — but the owner's D33 wording was specific to "the JSON editor of an existing message," and these two are a name/destination "Save As" picker, not a JSON editor; raised as a question for the owner in Outstanding Items rather than derived here. **TDD** (all four functions/files below are new production surface for this round — see the TDD Compliance table): four test files, 6+5+3 = 14 new assertions, all confirmed red for the correct reason before implementation, green after (the D31 test file's original branch-extraction helper had a bug in it — matched the wrong `requiresChoice` occurrence in `JsonModalBody.tsx`, since `commitLoad` also references `disposition.requiresChoice` earlier in the file — caught because its failure text quoted the wrong function body; the test was corrected to check full unique string fragments instead of a fragile slice, then re-verified genuinely red by `git stash`-ing the two source files, re-running, and stash-popping before trusting the green). **Verification**: `tsc --noEmit` 0 errors; `eslint` 0 errors/0 warnings on every changed/new source file individually (`formatMutationError.ts` needed two `--fix`-applied `import/order` corrections and a switch to a default export for `import/prefer-default-export`, both mechanical); `src/utils/index.ts`'s pre-existing `import/no-cycle` finding reproduced identically on the pre-round baseline via `git stash`/re-lint/`git stash pop` (unrelated to this round, not touched); full suite 595/595/0 (581 baseline + 14 new); the six specs referencing `JsonModalBody`/`NewMessageJsonInput` re-run individually for collateral drift, 39/39 green, none broken by the reorder or the `commitChoice` rewrite; `next build` exit 0/0/0, all nine routes generated. **Class sweep tables below.** Committed `website` `d7c4836` on `chain/013-load-notice-vessel`. Not pushed. Not sent to QA, code review, or UAT this round, per the owner's fresh waiver carried by steering. |

| 2026-08-25 | steering-hooks-013-2 (owner rejected the "found but not touched" flag; two corrections to the row above) | Correct two claims from the D31/D32/D33 row: the SaveAsMenu/NewMessageModal "parallel pattern," and the "Discord's own real API constraint" claim about `content: z.string().min(1)` | **Both corrections are mine to own, not steering's** — steering re-derived at source before relaying either one. **Correction 1 — the SaveAsMenu.tsx/NewMessageModal.tsx flag was wrong, and the owner rejected it** (verbatim: *"Why the fuck would the SaveAsMenu have the choice implemented if it's different than the others? There's no universe where SaveAs would encounter such a thing after the 013 is implemented. NewMessageModal does not work like how you're describing. It does not go to the next view or load. It correctly goes back to the now-resolved JSON..."*). I compared what each site's `commitChoice` **calls** (`submitData(...)` in both, matching D33's removed shape) and stopped there — I never checked what **triggers** each `setPendingChoice` call. In both files it is set in exactly one place, `onSubmit`, reached only after the user has already pressed Create/Save — a persist-time confirmation, not a parsing-effect side-effect. Submitting there on selection is correct, because the user asked to submit one click earlier; that is the opposite of what D33 fixed in `JsonModalBody`, where the choice was set from inside the JSON-parsing effect and fired before the user had asked for anything. "Two call sites call the same function" was a resemblance, not a relationship — I did not establish the trigger for either site before offering the finding. **No change wanted in either file. Neither was touched, before or after this correction** (steering independently verified: this round's only edits to those two files were the `formatMutationError` swap, byte-identical choice flow otherwise). Outstanding Items #12(b), which raised this as an open question, is withdrawn below, not silently deleted. **Correction 2 — "Discord's own real API constraint" was an unsupported claim, not an established fact.** Steering asked directly whether I had a source for `content: z.string().min(1)` reflecting a real Discord API limit, beyond the adjacent `.max(MAX_TEXT_DISPLAY_CONTENT_LENGTH)`. Re-checked `shapes.ts` to answer: **I did not.** The `min(1)` is a bare literal, no named constant, no comment, no test, no doc anywhere in this repo citing Discord's own behaviour for an empty Text Display. What I actually had was `MAX_TEXT_DISPLAY_CONTENT_LENGTH` being a named constant plus a general impression that this schema file mirrors Discord's documented limits elsewhere — I extended that pattern to `min(1)` without checking it specifically, then reported the inference as fact ("relaxing the server shape is not a viable option"). **That sentence is withdrawn — whether relaxing the server shape is viable is not established either way**, and DEFECT 1's remedy set is not narrowed to three options by anything I actually verified; it may still be exactly three, or it may not, pending whatever the owner or a further check establishes. **The diagnosis of the mechanism itself stands** — `createContainer()`/`createTextDisplay(content = '')` seeding an empty child, and no client-side validation catching it before Save, both directly read from source and unaffected by either correction. No source code was touched by this row; both corrections are to this document's own prior claims. |

| 2026-08-25 | steering-hooks-013-2 (owner ruling on DEFECT 1, D34, plan 013 rev 28, `515f845` — registration only) | Implement D34: relax the five emptiness floors in `shapes.ts` | **Owner's ruling, wider than the one constraint**: selection-prompt answer "Relax the server shape," chosen over blocking at the composer, stripping empty children, or leaving it — then two mid-turn widenings: "And relax other similar shapes" and "And relax other similar shapes, and tell me afterward." **Tree re-derived at round start**: `chain/013-load-notice-vessel` and `origin/master` both `d7c4836`, confirmed via `git status`/`git branch -vv`, matching steering's statement. **What changed**: `.min(1)` removed from exactly five fields in `src/server/api/utils/shapes.ts` — `actionRowShape.components`, `textDisplayShape.content`, `sectionShape.components`, `mediaGalleryShape.items`, `containerShape.components`. Every `.max(...)` on these same fields is untouched — named constants, out of scope by steering's own explicit statement. **Explicitly excluded, steering's own line, not the owner's (he named no exclusions)**: `containerShape.accent_color` (`z.number().int().min(0).max(0xffffff)` — a numeric range, not an emptiness floor; relaxing it would admit negative colours) and both `messageShape`/`partialMessageShape` `name` fields (`z.string().min(1).max(MAX_MESSAGE_NAME_LENGTH)` — the message NAME, not a component shape, already gated client-side by `disableSave = !messageName`; unnamed messages are a separate product decision not yet asked). Not touched, not derived, not widened on my own reading of "similar" — the boundary is exactly the five lines named. **Downstream load-bearing check, run before removing anything, per steering's explicit instruction**: `grep -rn "\.components\[0\]\|\.items\[0\]\|\.components\.length === 0\|components\[0\]\.content\|\.content\[0\]" src --include="*.ts" --include="*.tsx" \| grep -v __tests__` → 0 hits — no ordinal-indexing or non-emptiness assumption exists anywhere in this repo's own code for these five fields. Traced the two most plausible failure points directly: (1) `discordMessageImportShape`'s lenient filter (`filterSupportedComponents`/`filterTopLevelComponent`/`filterActionRowButtons`/`filterSection`, `shapes.ts`) already self-guards against producing an empty structure — `filterActionRowButtons` returns `null` (dropping the whole row) when `kept.length === 0`, and the Container branch of `filterTopLevelComponent` returns `null` when `children.length === 0` — so the import path gains no *new* empty-structure case from this change beyond a user hand-typing a literal empty array/string directly in the JSON editor, which was already reachable by the same route regardless of the filter. (2) `sendWebhook.ts`, the actual Discord dispatch path, does no local structural validation of `message.components` — it serializes and POSTs directly, so an empty array reaching it is a Discord-API question, not a local-crash question. **A concrete, non-hypothetical confirmation that these are genuinely reachable states, not just hand-crafted JSON edge cases**: `ContainerEditor.tsx`'s `deleteChild` (and the equivalent handlers in the other component editors) has no guard against deleting the last remaining child — the composer UI already permits reaching `components: []` on a live Container through ordinary clicking, with the server as the only thing that stopped it from being saved. **What remains genuinely unverified, and is written here as an open question, not a finding**: nobody in this chain has established whether Discord's own API accepts an empty Text Display, Container, Section, Media Gallery, or Action Row — the owner accepted this as a stated risk when choosing this remedy (described to him as moving a possible failure from a save-time 400 to send-time), and nothing checked this round narrows that unknown one way or the other. **No load-bearing downstream code was found — nothing to report as a finding beyond the two corrections already recorded above and this open question, which was already disclosed to the owner before he ruled.** **TDD**: `src/server/api/utils/__tests__/shapes-v2.test.ts` — 3 existing tests flipped from "rejects X" to "accepts X" (`rejects action row with zero buttons` → `accepts...`, `rejects text display with empty content` → `accepts...`, `rejects container with empty components` → `accepts...`), 3 new tests added for the two shapes with no prior coverage (section with zero text components, media gallery with zero items) plus a max-limits-unchanged regression control. ## Value Statement Validation

Original: *every editing surface around the composer … carr[ies] my message's components and its V2 flag faithfully … no editing action silently discards what I built.*

M1 delivers the data-layer half of that for Save As and the Message JSON view: both previously discarded every component, every flag, and most embed fields via `standardizeMessage`; both now carry the round-trippable form. Change detection (`hashMessage`) previously could not see a components-only or flags-only edit at all (it hashed a form that never included them); it now does, in both directions, modulo `_key`s and key order — closing D5's carried consequence, which the plan states is fixed nowhere else.

M8-G delivers the persist half, as of this pass: before this unit, `stripKeys` — the one function every real writer's payload passes through — silently deleted `content`/`embeds` whenever the V2 bit was set, with no notice and no choice, at every writer except the two JSON surfaces; a row-E draft (V2 components plus classic content, however it arose — including the F-1 population, a stored 5712/5842-shaped record opened in V2 mode and edited) could reach `message.upsert` and lose half its content with nothing shown. As of this pass, every real writer (`saveMessage`, Save As, Create) blocks on exactly that shape and asks, naming what each option drops, before anything is written; the serialiser itself no longer decides. Combined with M2/M7/M8-A–F (all prior units), the plan's value statement — "no editing action silently discards what I built" — now holds across the entire editing surface: both JSON surfaces on submission, and every persisting writer on save. What remains (M3, M4, M6) does not touch content-loss disposition; it is record-closure and residual-scope work per the plan's own text.

One line added (`"typecheck": "tsc --noEmit"`). This delta is pre-explained by the register itself; reconciling it here is confirmation, not a new finding.
- **`src/providers/MessageProvider/SaveChoiceModal.tsx`** — a **new production file**, not in the inventory, created at M8-G to hold the shared two-option/Cancel choice view `useApiMethods.saveMessage`'s row-E gate renders (a presentational component, `options`/`onChoose`/`onCancel` props, no logic of its own — the same view pattern `JsonModalBody.tsx` and `SaveAsMenu.tsx` render inline at their own two call sites). **Per steering's explicit instruction, this is recorded as a delta to explain, not written into the inventory as though authorised — its ratification is still with the owner, who has not ruled.** What it is: the M8-G persist gate's UI surface for the one writer (`saveMessage`, reached from `Navigation.tsx`'s Save item/hotkey and `edit/page.tsx`'s Save button/hotkey) that has no existing dialog of its own to render a choice branch inside, unlike `JsonModalBody.tsx`/`SaveAsMenu.tsx`/`NewMessageModal.tsx`, each of which already owns a `PresetDialog`. Why it exists: M8-G's own acceptance criterion requires the same row-E block-until-choice behaviour at every real persisting writer, and `saveMessage` is called from two places in the render tree (`Navigation.tsx`, `edit/page.tsx`) that don't share a common dialog host — rendering the choice once inside `MessageProvider`'s own `Provider`, as a sibling to `children`, was the mechanism that let one component serve both, rather than duplicating a third inline copy. Round 14's LOW-3 (routed, not fixed) already names the resulting triplication (`SaveChoiceModal` used only by `MessageProvider`; `SaveAsMenu`/`NewMessageModal` still carry inline copies) as the Preferred future fix — using `SaveChoiceModal` at all three sites — which would retire two of the three copies but not add a file the plan didn't already authorise.
- **Test files** — 14 test files touched or created (11 new, 3 existing files edited in place: `shapes-v2.test.ts`, `components-v2-data-layer.test.ts`, `mutual-exclusivity.test.ts`), all listed in Files Created/Files Modified above. **Not a delta against the 18-row table by design**: Plan Scope's file-opens inventory and count are explicitly about the files "this plan opens" for production behaviour (line 158's own justification is about seam/site count, not test coverage); the plan's separate Testing Strategy section names the expected test homes (`src/utils/__tests__/`, `src/server/api/utils/__tests__/`) without a count, and QA Phase 1/2 are the stages that evaluate coverage, not this reconciliation. Named here for completeness, not counted against the 18.
- **The four QA-owned re-anchoring files** (`012-json-editor-repair-path.test.ts`, `m7-m10-coverage-gaps.test.ts`, `012-message-mode.test.ts`, `012-send-gate-partition.test.ts`) — **confirmed untouched by the implementer**, consistent with the plan's own Plan Scope text naming them QA's, not this chain's, to edit. `src/pages/api/messages/codes/[code].ts` (the plan's one explicitly-named-and-not-opened reader) — **confirmed untouched**, consistent with Plan Scope's own text.

**Reconciliation result**: of the plan's 18 counted files, 16 carry a real diff, 2 (`shared.ts`, `bot/serverCommands.ts`) were investigated and confirmed to need no change — both disclosed at the milestone that investigated them, not newly surfaced here. Two files outside the inventory were touched: `package.json` (D21, pre-explained by the register) and `SaveChoiceModal.tsx` (new, explained above, ratification still owed to the owner — not absorbed as authorised). The conditional pair (`Navigation.tsx`, `edit/page.tsx`) was correctly not triggered. **No silent delta.** Derivation method: `git diff --stat c85945f2e847d1a9d0655fdc8f2dbad194e566a9 chain/013-components-v2-editing-surfaces -- src bot package.json` (the chain branch's merge-base with `master` to its current tip), a count computed by `git diff` itself, not through any output filter — 32 files total, 18 production (matching the plan's own 18 exactly, once the two investigated-no-change files are read as "opened, zero delta" rather than "not opened") plus 14 test files.

**Nothing from chain 012 is written in this document** — confirmed by re-scanning this implementation doc's own Files Modified/Created tables and changelog rows: every entry names a 013 milestone (M1–M8) or a code-review round against this chain; no row edits a 012-owned file for 012's own purposes (the four QA re-anchoring files above are 012's tests but are named here only as *not touched*, which is a fact about 013's scope, not 012 content).

**Correction (round 6 review, `d692206`, MEDIUM-1) to `applyDisposition`'s round-5 TDD row above**: that row overstates what was enforced. `tsconfig.json` excludes `**/__tests__` (`8f28066`, a Vercel-build fix — not to be reverted) and vitest does not typecheck, so round 5's "structural, unrepresentable" claim held only at the *value* level (an omitted argument reads as `undefined` at runtime), with **no type-level enforcement**. The reviewer executed the control directly: re-adding a required `callerDisposition: ContentLossDisposition` second parameter — the exact class round 5 deleted — still passes `tsc --noEmit` (exit 0) and the full spec (32/32), because the test file's callers are never typechecked. **Corrected classification: no test enforced the signature's structural closure before `a3e758b`.** Type-level enforcement of `applyDisposition`'s and `resolveContentLoss`'s signatures begins at `a3e758b`, where `JsonModalBody.tsx` — a typechecked production file — calls both directly; a regression to a wider, disposition-accepting signature now fails `tsc --noEmit` at that call site. `gateBeforePersist` has no production caller as of this pass and remains unenforced at the type level anywhere in the repo (see Outstanding Items).

**Note on M8-G's writer wiring (`useApiMethods.ts`, `SaveAsMenu.tsx`, `NewMessageModal.tsx`, this pass)**: same exemption class as the paste-surface note directly below and the `JsonModalBody.tsx` note further down — `saveMessage`/`commitSaveChoice`/`cancelSaveChoice` and each component's `onSubmit`/`commitChoice`/`cancelChoice` are React closures wiring already-tested pure functions (`gateBeforePersist`, `applyDisposition`, both covered above) into three new call sites, with no new pure logic of their own; no browser/component test harness exists in this repo to exercise them directly (Surfaces Without Automated Coverage, assumption 5). Their correctness rests on (a) the pure functions they call, each independently TDD-gated, and (b) direct reading plus the R9 class-sweep grep in the M8-G changelog row and Class Sweep Table above, confirming by inspection that every `upsertMessage`/`api.message.upsert` call at these three sites is preceded by a `gateBeforePersist` check, and that the only path to a write on the `mustAsk` branch is through `applyDisposition` after a choice. `stripKeys`'s own behaviour change (the one piece of new/changed pure logic in this delivery) **is** TDD-gated — see the table row above.

**Note on the paste-surface wiring (`NewMessageJsonInput.tsx`, this pass)**: no row is added to the table above — no new function or class was introduced. The unit wires four pure functions already TDD-gated at the M2+M8 Load wiring pass (`resolveContentLoss`, `applyDisposition` — table above, EC-3 rows; `noticeMessageForSettled`, `describeDropSet` — table above, "M2+M8 Load wiring" rows) into a second call site, unchanged. The TDD Gate Procedure's step 1 ("List all new functions/classes you will create") lists none for this unit, so steps 2–7 do not apply — this is not a "pure refactor" exemption claim (behaviour did change: row E now blocks and asks instead of silently stripping), it is that the new behaviour is entirely produced by functions whose correctness is already established by existing red/green evidence, called from a new site with no new logic of their own. The new site's own correctness — that it calls the right function with the right argument, in the right order, and gates `setMessage` the way M8-E requires — is not a claim a unit test on the pure functions could make; it rests on (a) the same "no browser/component test harness" limitation recorded for `JsonModalBody.tsx`'s `commitSettled`/`commitChoice`/`cancelChoice` below (Surfaces Without Automated Coverage, assumption 5) and (b) the R9 class-sweep grep in this pass's changelog row, confirming by direct inspection that both `setMessage` calls in the settled branch and both in the choice branch route through `applyDisposition`, and that `enforceImportMutualExclusivity` has zero remaining production callers.

**Note on the round-9 fix round's `reopenChoice` (`NewMessageJsonInput.tsx`)**: same exemption as the paste-surface wiring note above and the `JsonModalBody.tsx` note below — a React event-handler closure with no new pure logic (it moves existing state between two `useState` slots), not a new pure function; no browser/component test harness exists in this repo to exercise it directly. Its correctness rests on direct reading (this document's changelog row) and the `tsc`/`eslint`/full-suite/build results recorded there, which confirm nothing else in the file's type surface or behaviour regressed.

**Note on `commitSettled`/`commitChoice`/`cancelChoice` (`JsonModalBody.tsx`)**: not TDD-gated as their own units, for the same reason recorded below for `SaveAsMenu`/`JsonModalBody`'s M1 call-site edits — they are React event-handler closures inside a component this repo has no browser/component test harness for (Surfaces Without Automated Coverage, assumption 5), not new pure functions. Their correctness rests on (a) the pure functions they call (`resolveContentLoss`, `applyDisposition`, `noticeMessageForSettled`, `describeDropSet`), each independently TDD-gated and covered above, and (b) direct reading, quoted in this row's changelog entry and in Files Modified. M8-E's "nothing lands before the choice" property — the plan's own named instrument for this class — is verified by inspection here (`grep` in the changelog row above: exactly two `applyDisposition` call sites, one per `updateMessage` call, none preceding a choice) rather than by an executable test, matching M8-E's own stated instrument ("no `updateMessage`/`setMessageMode` call before the choice... each observed at its call site").

**Note on `canonicalize`**: not TDD-gated as its own unit because it is not itself a new public function or class — it exists solely to make `hashMessage`'s two red cases (key-order equality, `_key` equality) pass, and those cases were red-then-green against the real defect (`standardizeMessage`'s field-dropping), not against `canonicalize`'s absence. `SaveAsMenu`/`JsonModalBody`'s one-line call-site edits are not separately TDD-gated as new functions (they are not functions); their correctness is covered by the M1-b/M1-c round-trip property tests over the same `stripKeys`/`makeEditable` pipeline they now call, per the plan's own Testing Strategy and Surfaces Without Automated Coverage (no browser harness, assumption 5) — the component wiring itself is a one-line, no-new-logic change verified by direct reading (both call sites quoted in Files Modified above) rather than a UI test.

**Note on M4-a (`NewMessageModal.tsx`'s `submitData`, behaviour change to an existing function)**: not given its own TDD-gate row — the change is a one-line wrap of an already-tested pure function (`saveAsPayload`, TDD-gated at M1 fix round 1, table above) into an existing React closure, the same exemption class recorded above for `SaveAsMenu`/`JsonModalBody`'s M1 call-site edits and the M8-G writer-wiring note: no new pure logic is introduced, only a new call to an existing one, and this repo has no browser/component test harness to exercise the closure directly (Surfaces Without Automated Coverage, assumption 5). `saveAsPayload`'s own correctness (that it strips `_key`s and derives the V2 bit from structure without deleting content/embeds) is already established by its M1 tests, unchanged by this pass. Verified by direct reading (quoted in Files Modified) that `submitData` is `SaveAsMenu.tsx`'s `onSubmit` pattern exactly, line for line.

**Note on the M4-b/M4-c JSON-surface wiring (`NewMessageJsonInput.tsx`, `JsonModalBody.tsx`)**: same exemption class as the paste-surface and `JsonModalBody.tsx` wiring notes recorded above for M8 — `droppedComponentTypes`/`contentFloorError` state and their `NullableComponent` notices are React render-path changes over data already returned by TDD-gated pure functions (`describeUnsupportedComponentTypes`, table above; `validateContentRequirements`, pre-existing, unchanged by this pass), with no new logic of their own; no browser/component harness exists to exercise them directly. Correctness rests on (a) the pure functions' own TDD-gated coverage and (b) direct reading, quoted in Files Modified above — in particular, that `describeUnsupportedComponentTypes` is called on the raw parsed JSON before `discordMessageImportShape.parse` (so the notice reflects what the filter is about to drop, not what already vanished) at both surfaces identically.

**Correction (round 16 review, `dfc0f77`, HIGH-1) to the note directly above, for `JsonModalBody.tsx`'s M4-c gate specifically**: that note's "no new logic of their own" claim, applied to M4-c's `contentFloorError` gate, was **falsified** — the gate's addition changed `isDisabled`'s *reachability*, not merely its render text: because `validateContentRequirements`'s predicate is a strict superset of `resolveContentLoss(...).requiresChoice`, gating Load on `contentFloorError` (computed pre-disposition) made row E's own choice UI permanently unreachable, a genuine behaviour change no "render-path only" framing covers. This is not a case where the render-path exemption was wrongly claimed in general — `droppedComponentTypes`'s notice and `NewMessageModal.tsx:125`'s `contentFloorError` (swept above, confirmed not a member) remain correctly exempt on that basis — it is specific to gating a *reachable choice branch's visibility* on a wider predicate computed before the branch is chosen, which is a control-flow change, not a display change. The round-16 fix round (changelog row above) corrects this by moving the disposition check into the effect, ahead of the gate, so the two predicates can no longer race; no new pure function was introduced by that fix (the same TDD-gated functions are called from a new site), so no new table row is added, but this correction stands in place of the falsified claim rather than beside it.

**Correction (QA Phase 2, F-QA15, `agent-output/qa/013-components-v2-editing-surfaces-qa.md`, `c482584`) to the note directly above, for `JsonModalBody.tsx`'s M4-c gate a second time**: round 16's fix moved `resolveContentLoss` into the parsing effect so row E's own choice branch could not race the gate, but left `contentFloorError`'s own expression unchanged — `validateContentRequirements(validatedDraft)`, floored on the *unresolved* draft. Row D (V2 flag set, classic content, no V2 components — a warn-and-save-as-Classic disposition) sets the flag on a draft that still carries classic content; `validateContentRequirements` reads that combination as the `hasV2 && hasContent` error unconditionally, so the floor fired on every row-D draft and the Load button stayed permanently disabled for that row — a regression against base `c85945f`, where row D loaded successfully (QA's characterization, independently re-derived: I read `resolveContentLoss`'s row-D branch and `validateContentRequirements`'s predicate directly, confirmed the disjoint-checks framing was accurate, and only then applied the fix). Before touching the component, I closed QA's named "Assumption Not Checked" — that `applyDisposition` on a row-D draft produces a value `validateContentRequirements` accepts — empirically, over all eight row-D members enumerated in `013-fqa15-rowd-load-gate.test.ts`'s first `describe` block (assumption-closure test, run standalone before the fix): `applyDisposition` clears the V2 flag and preserves classic content for every member, and the resulting draft passes `validateContentRequirements` cleanly in every case (8/8), plus a losslessness check that no content is dropped in the process. Only with that precondition established did I change line 159–160 of `JsonModalBody.tsx` from `validateContentRequirements(validatedDraft)` to `validateContentRequirements(applyDisposition(validatedDraft))` — flooring the *resolved* draft (post-disposition) rather than the unresolved one, so row D's own resolution (which strips the conflicting flag) runs before the floor sees it. This matches QA's endorsed repair (its R3 mutant) exactly; I independently re-ran QA's own probe (`agent-output/verification/013/probe-qa-p2-rowd-load-gate/013-qa-p2-rowd-load-gate.test.ts`, copied transiently into the tree, run, then deleted — not committed) against the fixed source and reproduced its reported result byte-for-byte: 28/29 passed, the sole failure being the probe's own now-obsolete "unresolved draft" assertion (block D's regression anchor, which the probe itself documents as expected to flip once the correct fix lands). No new function was introduced — `applyDisposition` and `validateContentRequirements` are both pre-existing, independently TDD-gated (rows above); this is a second, corrected instance of the same wiring-site class the round-16 correction named, so no new table row is added, but this correction stands in place of the round-16 note's now-superseded account of this gate's behaviour, not beside it.

**Note on F-QA17 (`discordResponseToMessage.ts`'s `SUPPORTED_TOP_LEVEL_TYPES`/`SUPPORTED_CONTAINER_CHILD_TYPES`, coverage-only)**: no behaviour changed and no new function was introduced — the two sets were already set-equal to `shapes.ts`'s copies (confirmed directly against `discord-api-types`' real `ComponentType` enum before making any change). The only edit was adding `export` to both declarations so `013-fqa17-supported-types-parity.test.ts` could import and assert `toEqual` between the two copies directly, mirroring the M4-b precedent already used against the schema's own discriminants. TDD's red/green cycle does not apply to an `export` keyword with no logic behind it; the new test is coverage pinning an invariant that had none, not a gate on new behaviour.

**Note on F-QA18 (`hasV2Components`/`gateBeforePersist` and top-level Thumbnail, coverage-only)**: investigated as a possible code fix before writing any test, per steering's explicit instruction to stop and flag rather than absorb a product-behaviour change. `hasV2Components` (`src/utils/index.ts`) already checks against `V2_COMPONENT_TYPE_VALUES` (`src/constants/messageLimits.ts`), a set that already includes `11` (Thumbnail) — distinct from and broader than `shapes.ts`'s/`discordResponseToMessage.ts`'s `SUPPORTED_TOP_LEVEL_TYPES` (which correctly excludes 11, since a top-level Thumbnail is invalid Discord shape and gets filtered before either JSON surface's `gateBeforePersist` call). No code path was found where an unfiltered top-level Thumbnail reaches `gateBeforePersist` misclassified as non-V2. `013-fqa18-thumbnail-persist-gate.test.ts` (3 assertions, all passed on first run — no red phase, since no defect existed to make red) pins this: a classic-content draft carrying a raw top-level Thumbnail gates (row E), a pure-V2 Thumbnail-only draft does not, and a classic-only draft with no Thumbnail does not. Resolved as coverage-only; no finding of a scope or product-behaviour issue to report to steering.

**Note on the INFO-3 hole-check port (`164d720`, code review round 19, `agent-output/code-review/013-components-v2-editing-surfaces-code-review.md`, `ad7eee4`)**: no new function or class, and no red phase — the reviewer's own finding states this is "not a correctness defect in shipped code — the gate is correct today," a test-coverage gap rather than a present fault, matching F-QA18's exemption class above. The three added assertions are ported verbatim from the reviewer's own instrument (`verification/013/probe-cr21-fqa15-closure/fqa15-closure.cr21.test.ts`, CR21-C), already independently proven to pass against `53beb37`'s shipped gate; re-running them in `013-fqa15-rowd-load-gate.test.ts` at `164d720` confirms the same result in the project's own suite (15/15 in the file). Test-only commit, no source change.

**Correction (QA Complete, F-QA21, `agent-output/qa/013-components-v2-editing-surfaces-qa.md`, `c14e965`) to the note directly above and to this file's own two flag-clear assertions**: no new function was changed, but this **is** a genuinely red-then-green fix, not a further coverage-only addition — `expect(x.flags as number & 32768).toBe(0)` (both occurrences in the file) parses as a TypeScript type assertion, not a bitwise AND, and silently asserted `flags === 0` rather than "the V2 bit is cleared." QA's own mutation test demonstrates this was a real, previously-unguarded hole, not a cosmetic defect: a mutant that wipes every flag bit in `switchToClassic` (not just the V2 bit) **survived** against the pre-fix assertion (15/15 green — red-for-the-wrong-reason never fired) and is **killed** by the fix (16/16, `expected +0 to be 4`). Fixed to `((resolved.flags ?? 0) & FLAG).toBe(0)` at both sites, plus one new dedicated test asserting the property QA executed directly: `applyDisposition` on a draft additionally carrying `SUPPRESS_EMBEDS`(4)/`EPHEMERAL`(64) clears only the V2 bit, both other bits pinned to survive. TDD Gate applied to this fix: the mutant QA supplied (`next.flags = 0`) is the red-case evidence — re-run against the corrected assertions it fails as required (`expected +0 to be 4`), confirming the fix is genuinely coupled to the property it names, not merely re-parenthesised. `applyDisposition`/`switchToClassic` themselves are unchanged, pre-existing, independently TDD-gated (rows above) — only the spec's own assertion expression changed.

**Note (owner ruling 2026-08-25, toast-vessel fix round) — self-diagnosed TDD ordering lapse, compensating-evidence procedure**: implementation of the two conversions (`NewMessageJsonInput.tsx`'s `settledNotice` state, `MessageProvider/index.tsx`'s `codeMessageDroppedNotice` state plus `CodeMessageNotice.tsx`/`layout.tsx` wiring) was completed before `013-owner-toast-vessel-2026-08-25.test.ts` was written — a violation of mandated Red→Green, the same lapse this document previously recorded for `messageStore.store`. Method self-diagnosis, not smoothed over: the pressure to land the derived member-set analysis and the code together, in one pass, crowded out writing the test first — the same shape of lapse each time, worth naming as a pattern rather than a one-off. Handled via the established compensating-evidence procedure: `git stash push -- ` the three modified tracked files (`NewMessageJsonInput.tsx`, `MessageProvider/index.tsx`, `[id]/layout.tsx`), manually `mv`'d the new untracked `CodeMessageNotice.tsx` to `/tmp` (stash does not reach untracked files), ran the test against the genuinely-reverted tree: 7/9 assertions failed for confirmed-correct reasons (`AssertionError`s naming the exact still-present old code / still-absent new code; one `ENOENT` for the not-yet-existing `CodeMessageNotice.tsx`), 2/9 passed as expected (assertions on code the fix does *not* touch — `MessageProvider`'s four retained transient toasts, `JsonModalBody.tsx`'s retained toast — correctly true on the reverted tree too, since those two assertions target unchanged behaviour). Restored (`mv` the backup back, `git stash pop`), re-ran: 9/9 green. **This confirms the tests are coupled to the actual code paths they name, not vacuously true, closing the gap the ordering lapse opened.**

**Correction (QA, F-QA25, `agent-output/qa/013-components-v2-editing-surfaces-qa.md`, `ab83e3e`; restated beside the original claim per F-QA30 rather than overwriting it, this document's own earlier fix of F-QA25 having deleted the sentence it was correcting) to the sentence directly above.** I wrote that the stash-revert run "confirms the tests are coupled to the actual code paths they name, not vacuously true." **That overstates what the run establishes, and QA is right to have named it.** Red-on-a-reverted-tree is tautological for a `readFileSync` + `toContain`/`not.toContain` assertion: of course `not.toContain('toast.warn(notice)')` fails while the literal string `toast.warn(notice)` is still sitting in the file — the assertion is checking the file's text, and the revert changes exactly that text by construction. What the stash-revert procedure actually establishes is **text coupling** — that the assertion strings correctly identify the lines the fix touches — not **behavioural coupling**, i.e. not that the component actually renders differently, clears, or is styled when a live consumer calls it. Those are different properties, and only the second is the one a reader would reasonably take "TDD gate passed" to mean for a UI change. This is a correction to my own reasoning about what the compensating-evidence procedure proves, not a retraction of the procedure itself or of the disclosure — the ordering lapse was real, the stash/mv/restore mechanics were executed and reported accurately, and the procedure remains the right response to a text-assertion TDD gate specifically (it's what closes the gate for `013-fqa15-rowd-load-gate.test.ts`'s and this document's other source-text specs, where the property under test genuinely *is* the text). It stops being sufficient the moment the property that matters is behavioural rather than textual — which the toast-vessel round's actual subject (does the notice render, does it clear, is it styled) always was. See F-QA23/the adopted render instrument below for the behavioural half this note should have flagged as still open, rather than treating the text-coupling result as if it closed the gate.

| `JsonModalBody.tsx`/`NewMessageJsonInput.tsx` parsing-effect `requiresChoice` branch (D31 — `setDismissedChoice` in place of `setPendingChoice`) | `013-owner-conflict-choice-not-default-2026-08-25.test.ts` | Yes | Yes | 3/5 assertions red for the correct reason (source still contained `setPendingChoice({...})`, not `setDismissedChoice({...})`); the other 2/5 (the pre-existing, unchanged `reopenChoice` assertions) passed immediately, correctly — not part of what this round changed | Yes (5/5) |

**Note (self-diagnosed test-file bug, caught before trusting green, D31 row)**: the D31 test file's first draft used a brace-counting-free `parsingEffectBody()` helper (`src.indexOf('requiresChoice')` then slice to the next `return;`) to isolate the parsing effect's branch before asserting on it. Its first red run's failure text quoted `updateMessage({ data: next, updatedAt: Date.now() })` — that string lives in `commitLoad`, not the parsing effect — because `commitLoad` also references `disposition.requiresChoice` earlier in the file (line 70, `if (disposition.requiresChoice) return;`), and `indexOf` matched that occurrence first. The assertions were still checking real source text, so the run was red for a real (if mis-targeted) reason, not vacuously — but I did not trust it as clean TDD evidence once the mismatch was visible. Fixed the test to assert on unique whole-string fragments instead of a positional slice (`013-owner-choice-rewrites-editor-2026-08-25.test.ts`'s later `extractFunctionBody()` uses proper brace-depth counting from a unique function-signature anchor instead, avoiding the same class of bug). Re-verified genuinely red against the pre-fix source with the corrected test: `git stash push --keep-index` on the two source files, re-ran (3/3 red, this time quoting the correct branch), `git stash pop`, re-ran again (5/5 green). The corrected D31 row above reports that re-verified result, not the original mis-targeted one.

## Test Coverage

- **Unit**: `013-m1-transport-form.test.ts`, 7 tests — `hashMessage` both directions (4 tests: structural, flag-bit, component-field, isolating each conjunct where feasible; classicMessage fixture deliberately carries no content so the structural-only test isolates that conjunct) and both key-order/`_key`-equality negatives (2 tests); `stripKeys`/`makeEditable` round-trip for V2-components-only and classic-content-plus-username fixtures (2 tests, M1-b/M1-c instrument — **superseded as the M1-b/M1-c instrument by fix round 1's extraction tests below**, per MEDIUM-1; retained as coverage of `stripKeys`/`makeEditable` in their own right).
- `013-m1-fix-round-1-hash-parity.test.ts`, 6 tests (fix round 1, HIGH-1) — B1/B2/B3 producer/consumer parity (trim divergence in content, a button label, a thumbnail URL), a positive control (genuine V2 edit still mismatches), a `stripKeys`/flag-OR regression check, a no-throw check on an invalid draft.
- `013-m1-fix-round-1-extraction.test.ts`, 3 tests (fix round 1, MEDIUM-1) — `saveAsPayload` and `jsonModalSeed` each carry V2 components/flags and classic content/embeds/username faithfully. This is now the actual M1-b/M1-c instrument: both tests execute the exact expressions `SaveAsMenu.onSubmit` and `JsonModalBody`'s seed call.
- **Integration**: none added this pass — M1 has no tRPC-boundary surface of its own (the write boundary's shape is unchanged; only what the client sends before it gets there changes).
- `013-m8-ec3-resolver-gate.test.ts`, 29 tests after the fix round (M8, EC-3) — `resolveContentLoss` over all eight D17 cells, conjunct-isolation and both-directions variations, a row-E dropSet-absence assertion (MEDIUM-1), a MEDIUM-3 regression block (residue bit vs. classic content; combined bits; `undefined` flags) (18 tests); `gateBeforePersist` over a row-E draft, an ordinary classic draft, a pure-V2 draft, both row-E members regardless of the bit, and a residue-bit-only draft (5 tests); `applyDisposition` over settled rows A/C/D and both row-E chosen options, plus the no-option throw (6 tests). Expectations independence (EC-5): every expected row/mode/noticeClass/options value is a literal stated from the cell, never computed by calling the function under test; `applyDisposition`'s input dispositions are hand-authored literals matching each row's shape, not `resolveContentLoss`'s own output.
- `013-m7-mode-follows-flag.test.ts`, 16 tests (M7) — `isMessageV2` over all four cells of the `hasFlagV2 || hasStructuralV2` disjunction (conjunct-isolation, per QA's obligation table); `detectMessageMode` over the no-`components`-key case, the structural-only case, the classic case and the null-message case; `switchToV2`/`switchToClassic` over the D7-residue (other-bits-preserved) property, asserted directly; `applyModeSwitch`'s OQ-7 consequence (V2 Action-Rows-only reopens in V2) asserted **through `detectMessageMode`**, not through a direct `applyModeSwitch` import — **correction (round-1 review, `ecbbe43`)**: this spec does not directly exercise `applyModeSwitch`, contrary to what this bullet previously claimed; `applyModeSwitch` is covered, but only indirectly via `012-message-mode.test.ts` (QA's file, carrying this round's one predicted red) — see Code Quality Validation. `showV2PatchWarning` over its three-conjunct class (`showEdit`, `oldMessage`, `isMessageV2(message.data)`), each isolated as the sole carrier in one fixture. Expectations independence (EC-5): every expected value is a literal (`true`/`false`/a named flag constant), none computed by calling the predicate under test.

- `013-fqa17-supported-types-parity.test.ts`, 2 tests (QA Phase 2, F-QA17) — `SUPPORTED_TOP_LEVEL_TYPES` and `SUPPORTED_CONTAINER_CHILD_TYPES` asserted set-equal between `discordResponseToMessage.ts` and `shapes.ts`, mirroring the M4-b schema-parity pattern.
- `013-fqa18-thumbnail-persist-gate.test.ts`, 3 tests (QA Phase 2, F-QA18) — a raw, unfiltered top-level Thumbnail with classic content gates at `gateBeforePersist` (row E, not silently permitted); a Thumbnail-only pure-V2 draft does not gate (control); a classic-only draft with no Thumbnail does not gate (control).
- `013-owner-toast-vessel-2026-08-25.test.ts`, 9 tests (owner ruling 2026-08-25, toast-vessel fix round) — source-text assertions (no DOM harness in this repo, matching `013-fqa15-rowd-load-gate.test.ts`'s own technique): `NewMessageJsonInput.tsx` no longer calls `toast.warn(notice)`, no longer imports `react-toastify`, and exposes the settled notice as persistent `settledNotice` state rendered via `NullableComponent` (3 tests); `MessageProvider/index.tsx` no longer toasts the code-path dropped-components fact, still toasts its genuinely transient notices (query error, save-progress — not a blanket removal), and exposes `codeMessageDroppedNotice` on the `State` type/context (3 tests); `CodeMessageNotice.tsx` renders `codeMessageDroppedNotice` from `useMessage()`, and the shared `[id]/layout.tsx` renders it (2 tests); `JsonModalBody.tsx` still fires `toast.warn(notice)` for a settled Load disposition — asserted to confirm Site 1 is a deliberate non-member, not an oversight (1 test). **F-QA23 (QA, blocking): this spec pins source text only and cannot detect a behavioural regression** (proven by QA's mutants — see the next bullet); its role after the fix round is membership/wiring evidence, not the behavioural gate.
- `013-qa-toast-vessel-render.test.ts`, 13 tests (F-QA23 fix round, QA's own instrument, capture-and-adopt) — calls `NullableComponent`/`CodeMessageNotice` as plain functions, no DOM harness, D28-clean: `NullableComponent`'s truthiness contract across `'some notice'`/`null`/`undefined`/`''`/`false` (5 tests); `CodeMessageNotice` returns `null` with no fact, returns the notice element with the exact text when a fact is present, carries `text-warning` on a `div`, and clears when the fact is removed (4 tests); the paste surface's `!error && settledNotice` guard, conjunct-isolated through the real `NullableComponent` (4 tests). This is the behavioural half — mutating `CodeMessageNotice.tsx`'s `show` prop to always-`false` or its body to `''` (QA's R1/R4) kills this suite while leaving `013-owner-toast-vessel-2026-08-25.test.ts` fully green, which is the property F-QA23 named. Does not and cannot reach `NewMessageJsonInput.tsx`'s live render path (throws outside a renderer — a hooks-order artifact of `useCopyToClipboard`'s own `useRef`, documented in the instrument's own `trace-paste-surface-unreachable.txt`) or the async-server/client mount boundary — both routed to the owner (BC-18, BC-19's visual half, and a QA-stated Assumption Not Checked), not closable by any instrument this repo's D28 permits. **This "unreachable" conclusion was itself withdrawn by QA within the same day** — see the next bullet.
- `013-qa-paste-surface-render.test.ts`, 5 tests (F-QA28 fix round, QA's own instrument, capture-and-adopt) — against the **real, unmodified `NewMessageJsonInput`** (mocked hooks make its `useState` order deterministic; still D28-clean): renders the settled notice with no error, doesn't render with no notice, suppresses under an error (the `!error` conjunct isolated against the real component, not a replicated expression), carries `text-warning`, clears when the notice is removed. This is the render half of BC-18 the first QA pass at `abcf585` had recorded as unreachable without a harness D28 forbids — QA's own words: "a boundary claimed after one attempt is a hypothesis," withdrawn by building the instrument it had said couldn't exist. **BC-18 shrinks accordingly**: still the owner's (visibility, placement, the real Next.js mount); no longer his (renders at all, suppresses under error, clears, is styled).
- `013-owner-load-notice-reversal-2026-08-25.test.ts`, 4 tests (D29 reversal, owner ruling 2026-08-25) — source-text assertions on `JsonModalBody.tsx` (no DOM harness, matching the file's own established technique): no longer calls `toast.warn(notice)`; no longer imports `react-toastify`; computes `settledNotice` via `noticeMessageForSettled(disposition)` in the parsing effect and renders it through a `NullableComponent`; clears `settledNotice` alongside the effect's other per-edit state. Written and confirmed red before implementation — the genuine TDD Gate this round, no ordering lapse.

## Class Sweep Table (F-QA29 fix round — false `Yes` in a TDD row)

Trigger: QA found one TDD Compliance row answering `Test Written First? = Yes` while its own Failure Reason column opened "Implemented before the test was authored" — the one row-shape that defeats the TDD gate silently, since a disclosed `No` is auditable and an undisclosed/contradicted `Yes` isn't. The population swept is every row in the TDD Compliance table, not only the one QA named, since the same contradiction could exist elsewhere unflagged.

| Finding | Enumeration command | Full output (hit count + lines) | Disposition of every hit |
|---|---|---|---|
| F-QA29 — every TDD Compliance row whose own prose names an ordering lapse ("ordering lapse", "Implemented before", "preceded the test"), to confirm its `Test Written First?` column agrees with its own prose | `awk '/^## TDD Compliance/,/^## Test Coverage/' <doc> \| grep -n "ordering lapse\|preceded the test\|Implemented before"` (bounded to the TDD Compliance section specifically, not the whole document, so changelog/note prose using the same words elsewhere isn't miscounted as a row) | 6 lines matched: the `messageStore.store` row itself, its own explanatory note below the table, the two toast-vessel rows (`NewMessageJsonInput`, `MessageProvider`), the toast-vessel section's own explanatory note, and the F-QA25 correction paragraph (which discusses the lapse in the course of correcting a different claim, not as a row) | `messageStore.store` row — **the actual defect**: answered `Yes` while its own Failure Reason said "Implemented before the test was authored." **Fixed**: corrected to `No`, with the correction stated in place (F-QA29, this round). Its explanatory note below the table — prose, not a row, already accurately described the lapse; no column to be false in, not a hit. The two toast-vessel rows — already answered `No`, correctly matching their own "implementation preceded the test"/"same ordering lapse" prose; not a defect, confirmed consistent. The toast-vessel section's own explanatory note and the F-QA25 correction paragraph — both prose, not table rows with a `Yes`/`No` column; not members of the class this finding names. |

Post-fix re-run of the same enumeration command: same 6 lines (the fix corrected a column value, not the surrounding prose, so the grep's hit set is unchanged) — the `messageStore.store` row's `Test Written First?` column now reads `No`, matching its own prose; every other hit remains a non-member for the reason recorded above. 9. **REVERSED by direct owner ruling, same day — see item 11 below and the D29-reversal changelog row. Kept unedited as the record of this session's own reasoning at the time it was ratified (D29), not because the reasoning still holds.** Owner ruling 2026-08-25 (toast-vessel fix round) — deliberate finding stated in full: `JsonModalBody.tsx`'s Load notice (Site 1, "the Load one") is left as a toast, not converted, and this is a considered outcome against the owner's own refined test, not an oversight or a partial implementation. The owner's refined test: "if the action is not transient then the message should be persistent... if the error is not stateful but transient, then a toast might be correct... reporting on an action where the state cannot be represented persistently." Applied directly: `commitLoad`'s `toast.warn(notice)` (line 72) reports that a settled disposition (row B/D) was applied *while loading* — the fact that the load-action changed something on the way in — not a durable state the surface keeps showing afterward; the function disposes immediately (`onClose()` a few lines later), so there is no "after" left mounted for a persistent notice to attach to. Checked for a durable home before concluding this, not assumed absent: `showV2PatchWarning` (`src/utils/messageMode.ts`) is narrow to the re-editing-a-previously-sent-message flow at `send/page.tsx` only (`showEdit && !!oldMessage && !!message && isMessageV2(message.data)`), and a repo-wide grep of `_layout/*.tsx` found no general V2/Classic mode indicator anywhere in the editor that this fact could attach to without inventing new UI machinery beyond what "reevaluate the vessel" asked for. This conclusion was reached independently, via the test itself applied to this specific site, not by accepting steering's own earlier framing (which asserted the opposite and was explicitly retracted by steering once the owner's refinement arrived) — steering's retraction is recorded verbatim in this round's changelog row. A future pass that adds a durable per-draft state indicator to the editor (which nothing here proposes) would reopen this specific conclusion, since the "no durable home exists" premise would no longer hold; until then, this is the correct-per-test outcome, not a gap.
